The bundle is **committed** and embedded in the node binary as the dashboard's files are: `include_str!` of each file of `apps/dashboard/` into a static list of (name, text) in `apps/live.rs`, served by `apps::serve`; the client's bundle is one more such list (`include_bytes!` for binary assets). Stitch stays a single binary, and cargo, CI and the iOS builds need no JS toolchain. Proposed: a CI check that rebuilding the bundle gives the committed files. The built-in dashboard became a view of the client on 2026-11-04 (§2.10, "The dashboard is the client's home view"): a second entry of the same bundle.
- **The protocol is public** (decided 2026-11-02, with the user), with two rules.
  - *Types:* TypeScript types are generated from the Rust protocol types with **ts-rs** (stable 12.x; specta 2 is still a release candidate), and the generated `.ts` (one file per type in `apps/client/src/api/`, `just client-types`, behind stitch-node's off-by-default `ts` feature so the iOS build does not link ts-rs, large integers as `number`) is committed, so the client's `tsc` breaks when Rust changes and every protocol change shows as a diff. No JSON Schema step (schemars and converters) until an outside consumer needs one. The WebSocket carries the same serde types as JSON. The trap: postcard (the socket) is not self-describing, so `skip_serializing_if` must not be used on these types.
  - *Version:* unchanged, an exact match. `Request::Hello { version }` opens a connection, and `VERSION` (`lib.rs`: the crate's version and `local-N`, here `local-52`) must equal the node's; `API_SHAPE`, a hash of `api.rs`'s code, and a test in `api.rs` fail when the API changes without `local-N` bumped. The build (`set_build`, §2.1) is separate and says which code runs. The client is embedded in the node, so always of its build: the pin ("Stitch", `builtin` `client`) syncs between nodes of different builds, so a node serves a client pin **from the bundle in its own binary, whatever manifest the pin holds** (`live::serving_manifest`), and `ensure_client` moves the pin only for an older `CLIENT_VERSION` (never between builds of the same one, which would flip it back and forth). On a mismatch (the node restarted into another build) a stale tab reloads itself **once per node version** (a `sessionStorage` guard: the bridge leaves the tab's own `sessionStorage` to pages, which lasts across reloads; until 2026-11-17 it put an in-memory one in its place, so the guard never held and every tab reloaded for ever on a mismatch, the node serving each reload at a core's cost; tested in `web::live_tests`); still mismatched, it stops and shows "This page's client (X) doesn't match the node (Y): reload" instead of looping (decided 2026-11-03: a fleet on mixed builds had every tab reloading forever against a node that served another build's bundle). Tested with `a_client_pin_of_another_bundle_serves_this_binarys_app`. The Discord bridge ships in the same image, so it matches too. Version windows (current plus previous) wait for a real outside client.
- **Built (2026-11-02, local-50; branch `web-client-v1`).** The WebSocket (`apps/ws.rs`) gives each request that has replies a lane of its own (a connection to `node::serve` with its own pipe), so every frame carries its request's `id` by construction; requests that answer nothing are written to the chat they act on (`Approval` to the chat that asked, others by an optional `"to": <chat request id>`, else the newest chat). `Request::Threads` and `Request::Transcript` reply with `Reply::Lines([json])` built with `json!`, so their payload types are written by hand in `apps/client/src/payload.ts`; `Transcript` pages at 500 entries (`next`). The client pin is hidden from `Request::Apps` (devices have their own UI) and opened by name (`AppOpen "Stitch"`, `stitch app open Stitch`); `ensure_client` re-pins when the embedded bundle differs. The bundle is `apps/client/dist/`, embedded by `include_str!`; `just quick` rebuilds it (needs bun) only when `apps/client` changed. **A lazily loaded chunk (2026-11-12, Space, §2.25):** the client's bundle is an IIFE (`app.js`), which bun cannot split; what is big and rarely needed is a second entry built as an **ES module with a stable name** (`bun build src/map/space/entry.ts --format esm` → `dist/space.js`: three.js and the 3D engine, ~576 KB, ~146 KB gzipped), listed in `live::CLIENT` beside `app.js` (so it is in the pin's snapshot and served by `apps::serve` as any file of the app, `text/javascript`, same origin, allowed by `script-src {own}`), and loaded by a native `import()` of a URL built at run time (`map/space/load.ts`; the bundler leaves it alone) the first time 3D is shown, with `?v=<CLIENT_VERSION>` so a cached chunk of another build is never used. The chunk shares no code with `app.js` at run time: it gets everything through an interface (`map/space/types.ts`, types only), and must never import the store or the signals (a second copy). `app.js` grew by ~4 KB (~1.3 KB gzipped) for the switch, the loader and the hand-over; the dashboard entry is unchanged. A failed load or no WebGL 2 says so in the map and leaves 2D working; going back to 2D lets the next 3D try again.
- **Home, tree sidebar, spawn panel (batch 2b, local-51; branch `client-home`).** `Request::Live { what, query }` (`attention`, `jobs`, `schedules`, `loose-ends`; `Reply::Lines([json])`, the functions of `apps/live.rs` and `loose::live`, no copy) completes the dashboard's data; loose ends are checked and dismissed with the existing `LooseEnds`; the socket's `changed` push already fires for all four kinds. The client shows a home view (needs you with Allow/Always/Decline inline, what's going on with in-flight steps and jobs, scheduled, loose ends, timeline) when no thread is open or the app title is clicked, a sidebar of spawn trees (collapsed unless working or needing you, a "needs you" badge, the filter flat over all threads) and a hideable right panel of the open thread's spawned threads, jobs and tree cost (`src/home`, `src/sidebar`, `src/panel`).
- **Rich transcript (2026-11-02, local-51; branch `client-rich`).** `apps/blob.rs`: `GET /_stitch/blob/<hash>` serves images and `POST /_stitch/blob` stores a pasted or dropped one (answers `{hash, type}`), for the client's origin alone (Host, cookie, pin = client, `Origin` and `Sec-Fetch-Site` same-origin; a POST needs its own `Origin`); images only, decided by magic bytes (never SVG), with `nosniff` and CORP same-origin. `Request::Chat`/`Interject` `attachments` also take `blake3:<hash>` (an image already in the store), which is how the client sends images (`Send` has no attachments, so a message with images is a `Chat` turn). `Request::Transcript` tool entries gain `images` (what view_file, screenshots and generate_image showed, found in the "From <tool> (call <id>)" message after the results, which is no longer an entry of its own), `app` (`{manifest, name}` of `show_app`; the client resolves it with `AppOpen` on the manifest) and `diff` (`{path, text}` of `edit_file`/`write_file`); `result.output` is the tool's text, not its stored JSON. Apps are iframes on their own origins (the client's CSP `frame-src` allows `http://*.localhost:*`), sandboxed, with no bridge to the client; there is no resize message in the apps bridge, so their height is fixed (taller on a button). A token URL opened by an iframe (`Sec-Fetch-Dest: iframe`) sets its cookie `SameSite=None; Secure; Partitioned`, as a Lax one is not sent cross-site.
- **Needs you, clearable; continuing on another node (batch 3a, no protocol change; branch `client-needs`).** The node computes the threads that need the user once (`live::needs_threads`, in `Request::Live attention` as `threads`: a thread `blocked` without an approval of its own, or idle with its last report `blocked`), and the client, the dashboard and the count share it. A spawned thread whose blocked report went to its parent (a parent that exists, report not `wake = false`) is not the user's (the parent has it); a parentless or kept one is. "Dismiss" is the loose end's (`LooseEnds { dismiss }`, a user `set` of `loose-end-dismissed` = the thread's last step: a new step brings it back, one record for both lists). A schedule paused by its node is dismissed by the user pausing it (`Schedule` `pause`, no longer "by the node"), or resumed (`resume`). The client types into a thread another node runs as a `Chat`, and **by default that node is the thread's owner** (decided 2026-11-02, the user): the WebSocket's connections are `Peer::Web` (the user, as `App`), and for them `chat_steered` treats a thread with no `runs-on` choice whose owner (`weave::runner_in`: the node of its latest segment) is another node that runs programs as pinned to that owner (`weave::owner_elsewhere`), so `elsewhere::chat` passes the message there and streams the turn back on the same request; the client shows the replies itself (nothing local follows it). "Run here" is `RunOn` none (an explicit choice, so the default no longer applies); "Continue on…" is `RunOn <node>`. The in-process app (iOS) has the same default (§2.7; `Peer::owner_default`); the TUI keeps running an unpinned thread where it is typed. The header says "Runs on <runner>" or "Runs here" (the list's `node` is the runner). Tested: `the_web_client_continues_a_foreign_thread_on_its_owner_by_default` (two nodes, a real WebSocket), and by hand in headless Chrome on two throwaway nodes.
- **Backpressure (2026-11-02; branch `ws-backpressure`).** Incident: the client re-listed (a full `Threads` plus four `Live`) on every `changed` push, without waiting for the last round, each request on its own task; on a swapping machine the list reads (blocking tasks that all queue on the index lock) filled tokio's blocking pool (512 threads), starving every tool, while the Unix socket's CLI still answered. Why `changed` fired so often: the watch's marks for threads, attention and schedules each included the count and last row of **transcript segments**, so every model step of every running thread moved all three, and the threads mark also moved with every `set` record (any app's state write). Now: (1) *client:* `refresh` and `refreshLive` are one in flight and one queued (`coalesce.ts`); `changed` is debounced 500 ms, at most 2 s; the four `Live` reads only while the home view is shown (an open thread reads `jobs`, and `attention` once, for the node's name); transcript reloads from a stream (tool results, gaps, turn end) are debounced 300 ms / 1.5 s. (2) *`ws.rs`:* a connection runs 8 one-reply requests at once, 64 more wait as futures, the rest get `{"id", "error": "busy: …"}`; `changed` goes out at most once per 500 ms with a trailing push. (3) *node:* attention and schedules marks no longer include segments (`Index::mark_records`); the marks' index reads run on `spawn_blocking`; `Threads`, `Live` and `Transcript` take one of 4 permits (`Shared::list_read`) before using the blocking pool, so waiters are futures, not threads; `live::threads` builds no row past its limit when no status filter applies. `live::threads` and `attention` never hold the index lock across slow work (it is taken per item). Open: `changed` still fires on every `set` and every segment for threads (the list shows `last_step`).
- **Deltas, not re-fetches (decided 2026-11-15, with the user: "deltas/partials and reactive, not changed → re-fetch everything").** Incident: on gamer the node read ~600 MB/s from the index and took ~1.1 cores while a web client sat on the home view and threads worked; every `changed` (a running call moved the threads' mark, so about every 1.5 s) re-read the whole list (`Threads` 300, ~0.4 MB, 150–300 ms), each open place section (~0.7 MB each), the four live sections and, with the map shown, the forest (2 MB). The index side was fixed first (2026-11-14: a 64 MB page cache, the `place` records shared until the index changes, `edges_rel`). Now the WebSocket pushes what changed, and the client applies it to its store. *Protocol* (ws-only frames, no `api.rs` change, so no `local-N` bump; the iOS app does not use the WebSocket, its views read through the FFI as before): the client sends `{"watch": {"limit": N}}` after `Hello`; from then that connection gets no `changed`, but `{"list": {"v": 1, "reset": <Threads reply>}}` at once, then `{"list": {"v": n, "upsert": [rows, whole], "remove": [ids], "places"?: [counts]}}`, each `v` the last plus one; a reset again (`v` going on) when the node cannot say a delta (rows removed from the index, a `place`, `project`, `folder` or read floor written, or the connection fell behind the feed's channel). A client that sees a `v` out of order sends `watch` again (a resync, from 1). `{"live": {"what": "attention"|"jobs"|"schedules"|"loose-ends", "value"}}` brings a section whole when its mark moved (loose ends at most every 10 s: every step moves them). `{"calls": {"threads": [...]}}` names the threads whose running calls the connection hears as `{"call": {"thread", "in_flight": {name, headline, summary, since}|null}}`: a running call is not a list change (the client watches the threads shown working and the open one). A client that sends no `watch` (an older tab) keeps `changed`. *Node* (`apps/feed.rs`): one pass a second in the live watch, shared by every connection, only while one watches: the threads that moved are those named by what the index took in since the last pass (`Index::cursor`, `records_since`, `segment_threads_since`: a thread record, a record's edge targets and their `thread`, a segment's thread), and those whose loop started or ended here; their rows alone are built (`live::threads` with `ids=`, which reads no other thread), mapped to their compaction chain's newest; the counts per place again only when threads came or went. *Client* (`deltas.ts`, pure, `test/deltas.test.ts`): rows put in place in the window (a new one only when it falls in it), in the place sections by their place (a row that moved elsewhere leaves a section), in the open thread's row, and in the map's forest (laid out when it is shown); `earlier` and `remove` take threads out; the 60 s re-read of the live sections stays (times move on). Tested: `a_running_call_is_no_list_traffic_and_a_row_change_is_one_delta` (`ws.rs`). Measured (`bench_home_view_while_a_thread_works`, ignored, on a copy of gamer's data, release, a call every 1.5 s and a row change every 4.5 s, the home view without place sections or the map): 96.7 KB/s to the client before, 4.3 KB/s after; the process's CPU 11.6% → 9.7% of a core (most of it the node's own work, not the list). The live node's cost came mostly from what this leaves out (sections, the map, several tabs), each now a delta too. *Not covered:* a parent's title shown in its children's rows (`parent_title`) follows only when a child's row moves.
- **Sending feedback and the turn's status (2026-11-03; branch `web-send`).** Report: after a message was sent nothing showed the turn worked until, much later, the message appeared queued or the reply came. Causes: (1) a turn the node starts (`weave::send` → `detached::turn_with`) runs a preamble before its loop begins (`Steering::new`, which pushes `Started` and makes `threads` say `working`): `weave::catch_up` syncs the thread with **every** peer first, 5 s each in parallel, so one unreachable peer (a sleeping phone) costs 5 s, then the model, its key, the start directory and the tools; meanwhile no signal existed, and the client cleared the message's "sending" at once on `Send`'s reply. (2) The client chose `interject` from the list's `state`, debounced (500 ms–2 s) and stale: a message sent while the list still said idle became a turn of its own, waiting on the thread's lock for the turn running, written to the transcript only when it began; a transcript read meanwhile dropped the optimistic entry, so the message vanished and came back late. (3) `Send` (and every user action) was a one-reply request behind the connection's 8 slots, so it waited behind a burst of refreshes, or was refused `busy`. Now: *node:* `Live::queue`/`unqueue` count, per thread, the turns accepted whose loop has not begun (`QueuedTurn`, from `turn_with` until `Steering::new`, or the task's end); `Live::status` is `{running, queued}`, and `threads` says `working` ("a turn is starting") while one is queued; a follow on the WebSocket gets `{"event": {"thread", "turn": {"running", "queued"}}}` when it starts and on each change (a ws-only frame: no `api.rs` change, nothing between nodes). `ws.rs` gates only reads (`gated`: lists, transcripts, live data); the user's actions run at once, bounded apart (`ACTIONS`, 32). *client:* `Send` always with `interject` (the node weaves it into the loop running, else starts a turn, queued behind one starting); a message sent stays in the transcript, after the node's entries, until the node's transcript has it (`transcript/outbox.ts`, tested with `bun test` in `just client`), with its state under it: sending, starting a turn, queued (runs after the current turn), queued for the running turn's next step (until its `Note` comes back), or "not sent: why" (an error frame such as `busy`, no answer in 30 s, the socket closed). The working line is driven by the node's status: "Starting the turn…" while queued, the turn's phase while running, "N messages queued after it". The preamble's catch-up itself was shortened after (§2.7, "How long a turn's start waits on a peer": 1 second on the local network, the rest in the background).
- **Opening a thread older than the list (2026-11-03, local-52; branch `web-open`).** Report: a search hit on an older thread did nothing visible. The list is windowed (`Threads { limit: 300 }`, the latest first; gamer knows ~1000 threads), and the open thread's row was looked up in it alone, so a thread past the window left `current` empty and the view fell back to home. Now `Request::Threads` takes an optional `thread` (and, from local-56, an optional `place` and replies with per-place counts, §2.25) (`live::threads`' `id=`): that thread's row alone, whatever the limit (none when unknown or deleted). The client reads it when it opens a thread the list lacks and again on each list refresh (`store.ts` `extra`, only the open thread's row kept), so refreshes do not drop it; while it is read the transcript shows under a "Loading the thread…" header, and "No such thread" when the node has none. The sidebar stays windowed. Tested: `one_threads_row_is_read_past_the_lists_limit` (`tests/client_ws.rs`), `apps/client/test/open.test.ts`, and in headless Chrome on a throwaway node with 306 threads (a search hit on the oldest opens it and it stays open through refreshes).
- **Long conversations open at their end (2026-11-22, local-73; branch `tail-window`).** Report: a conversation compacted eight times (11.6k events, 17 MB of segments) made the web client slow. Opening it read 12 `Transcript` pages (~8 MB of JSON; each page rebuilt the whole lineage on the node before skipping to `after`) and rendered every entry, tool output included (55k DOM nodes, ~5 s); then every list push and stream delta re-rendered all of them (each message read the row's `current`, a new object on every push), and the markdown cache (cleared past 500 texts) re-parsed every message each time. Now: `Request::Transcript` takes `tail` (and `before`): one window, the last `tail` entries before `before`, cut between events (a divider stays with its event), saying `start` (the `n` of its first entry) and `earlier`; the client opens the last 300 entries, **Show earlier** reads the window before (the view kept where it was), a reload after a turn reads from the window's start (`after`), a rewind before it the end anew, and a digest item or a search hit reads back until its message is there (`S.reveal`). Rendering: each entry is a component rendered again only when it changes (`Row`, keyed by its event's `n`, not its position), messages read only the row's ID and whether it works (`currentId`, `currentWorking`), and the markdown cache keeps 2000 texts, least recently used out. That thread opens with one 457 KB read and 4.3k DOM nodes. *Not done:* tool output is still in the DOM, folded (built on opening it, and a capped preview in the transcript, would cut the bytes too); a reload still reads the whole window rather than only what is new.
- **Coming back to a thread mid-turn (2026-11-08; branch `web-live`).** Report: a thread left while a call ran or waited (an approval) showed none of the turn's calls on coming back, and a message woven into a turn sometimes stayed as a bubble at the transcript's end until another thread was opened and this one again. Causes: (1) a step is written once its calls return; meanwhile it lives only in what the loop keeps (`keep_pending`), and following starts from now, so a client opening the thread (or reloading it on a tool result) read none of it; (2) the messages sent from here lived only in the open transcript's entries, cleared on switching; (3) the node writes messages that came in a row from one sender as one, while a bubble went only on an entry of exactly its text; and a turn reading a woven message before `Send`'s answer came left it waiting. Now: `Request::Transcript` ends with the step a live loop here runs the calls of (`chat::step_in_progress`, this process's alone, not counted in `next`, events already written dropped), the running call without a result; the client keeps each thread's unwritten messages across switches (30 minutes at most), a bubble goes on its run of whole lines in such a message, and a note matches a message still `sending`. Not covered: the text a model call streams before its step exists.
- **Hash routes, the Map | List switch, d3 (2026-11-12, local-62; branch `reading`).** The client had no URL routing; it has three hash routes and no router library (`route.ts` pure, `nav.ts` the history): `#/` (home: the remembered Map | List), `#/map` (home, the map; it also sets the remembered choice), `#/t/<thread id>` (a conversation), with `?from=map` when it was opened from the map. Every way of opening a conversation (sidebar row, search hit `A.openHit`, notification and dashboard links through `initLive`'s `open`, the spawn panel, a digest item, the compaction links, `?mock&open=`) is `store.select`, which calls `nav.opening` first, so the URL and its history entry follow; `goHome` likewise (`homing`). The browser's back and forward and a reload come back through `nav.start` → `store.applyRoute` (selecting without writing a second entry). A conversation opened from the map (the map was on screen) has a **← Map** button in its header; each history entry records its distance to the map's entry (`history.state.d`: 1 from the map, +1 for a thread opened from that conversation: the spawn panel), so the button is `history.go(-d)` and lands on exactly that entry; with no such state (a pasted link) it replaces the entry by `#/map`. Esc does the same unless a layer is open (the thread menu, a dialog, the lightbox) or the focus is in a field. Home has a **Map | List** switch (`stitch.homeMode` in localStorage, default List); List is the home view as before. With the map shown the sidebar has a « button that folds it away (`stitch.sidebar.collapsed`; a ☰ in the map's header brings it back; a conversation shows the sidebar again). **Dependencies** (exact pins, `bun.lock`): `d3-hierarchy` 3.1.3, `d3-zoom` 3.0.1, `d3-selection` 3.0.1, `d3-transition` 3.0.2, `d3-ease` 3.0.2, and their `@types/` as dev dependencies; `d3-interpolate` and `d3-quadtree` are not needed. Bundle `app.js` 203,825 → 307,072 bytes (gzip 68,672 → 105,696; `app.css` 25,382 → 31,307); the dashboard's bundle is unchanged (51,759).

- **Pinned apps, the switch's place, a pending thread (2026-11-13).** The sidebar has an **Apps** section above Pinned (closed by default, `stitch.apps` in localStorage; hidden while filtering and when no app is pinned): `Request::Apps` read on connecting and on each opening, sorted by name, each a chip that opens the app in a tab of its own (its `url`, else `AppOpen`; the tab is opened before the await so popup blockers let it through). The built-in client stays out of the list (as before). The Map | List switch is first in both home headers (`order: -1`, the list header padded to the map header's 56 px), so it does not move when it flips the view. A new thread's pending view (`creating`) ends when the node names the thread, not when its first turn ends (`Chat` streams until `ChatDone`), and any navigation clears it: before, home or ＋ during a first turn showed the pending "Starting the thread…" screen; a thread named after the user went elsewhere no longer pulls them into it. **Named, not yet written** (2026-11-20, the user: "I send the message, it goes on top, and nothing happens for a while"): the node writes a new thread with its first step, so for that step (a long first answer: 40–70 s of thinking, measured) neither the list nor `Threads { thread }` had it, and the opened thread showed "No such thread" over the message with no working indicator nor Cancel (the follow starts after `Started`, and `Thinking` is not in the transcript). The client now keeps a provisional row from the `Thread` reply (`store.ts` `provisional`: titled from the message, `working`) until the node lists it or the chat ends, so the view is the thread's own from the start, "Working…" then "Thinking…". Not done: the sidebar lists it only once written. **Resizing the map** (the sidebar folded or unfolded, the window): Islands keeps what the view was last framed on (`framed`, a transform computed again for the new size: the whole map, a place, an effort, a thread) and frames it again; a view moved by hand keeps its center and zoom. Before, any focus (a click on a place) left the view anchored at the left edge. Space needs nothing: its camera looks at its target through the canvas's center, and a resize sets the aspect.

- **App pin management:** an app's frame can pin it (following newer versions from that thread); each entry in the sidebar's **Apps** section can also be unpinned directly, by pin ID. This does not need the originating conversation or a locally available version, and removes only the pin.
- **Chat model picker:** the first level keeps the automatic choice, default and quick together with the provider list. Opening a provider shows only its models, with a Back action; selections still send the full model ID. The same picker serves new and existing chats.

- **Pinned and shown apps stay on their own origins** as iframes in the client (decided), sharing nothing with it; the dashboard becomes one view of the client.
- **What it must do well** (decided), where the TUI is weak: images (`img:` IDs, zoom, the editing viewer), apps inline (`Reply::App`), rendered markdown and diffs, threads side by side and spawn trees, approvals as buttons, the browser co-op session (§2.18), and notifications (the Notification API; `localhost` is a secure context).

**Capability tiers** (proposed): the one new piece of policy. (1) *Ordinary:* its own state and, by §2.22's grant, its family. (2) *Read live state:* `stitch.live`, by `allow-live` or the pin's `builtin`, as the dashboard. (3) *Act as the user:* the client's reach above. Tier 3 is **a grant of §2.22's kind**, not a new mechanism: a `set` on the app's conversation (a field beside `threads:<name>`), its value the needs and their hash, written only by `Author::user` and read as `Index::current_user_value`; given by trusted UI that **shows** the needs, then writes with `--needs <hash>` (as `allow-threads`), refused when the version has other needs by then; an agent's shell asks first; a changed manifest asks again. It is **never available to a page `show_app` made**: the node refuses to show or write it for one, since the model that wrote the page is not the user. The built-in client holds it by its pin (`builtin = "client"`, moved with the binary as the dashboard's is), no grant asked. Enforcement is the node's, per call, from records, as §2.22's guard is; iframes embedded in the client get none of its authority (a narrow message set for resize and "open thread" only, no bridge for requests).

**No service worker in v1** (decided 2026-11-02, with the user). Chrome installs a PWA from the manifest alone. The WebSocket reconnects with backoff and shows a "node restarting" banner, which covers deploys (canary and fleet restarts, [self-improve.md](self-improve.md)). Notifications use the Notification API while the window is open and the node's own desktop notification (`notify`) while it is closed; no Web Push. A shell-only service worker (an offline page saying how to start the node) is a possible later step.

**Platforms:** Windows is out of scope (decided). The node runs only on Linux and macOS; on Windows it runs in WSL2, and WSL's localhost forwarding lets a Windows browser reach the client.

**License** (decided 2026-11-02, with the user; **applied 2026-11-22**). The node, the TUI and the web client are under **FSL-1.1-ALv2** (no competing use for two years, then each version becomes Apache-2.0): `LICENSE` is the fsl.software template, verbatim, Licensor Fragcolor Pte. Ltd.; every crate says `license = "FSL-1.1-ALv2"` (the SPDX identifier, since 2026-11-22; it was a `LicenseRef-`) and `publish = false`. Otter and Engram stay under MIT OR Apache-2.0 (corrected 2026-11-21; Engram is public too). MeshRepair (GPL-3, and embedded in the closed app through `stitch-ffi`) was replaced by our own implementation before the source was opened: the `meshrepair` repository, MIT OR Apache-2.0. `stitch-ffi` links the node's crates into the closed iOS and visionOS app: the licensor's own use, which the FSL does not restrict; so that it stays clean, outside contributions come under a CLA (`CONTRIBUTING.md`, `.github/CLA.md`, signed through CLA Assistant), which grants Fragcolor the right to relicense (the two-year Apache-2.0 grant, proprietary clients). The iOS and visionOS apps and the hosted services (the push relay's deployment, the vault service, scheduling while machines sleep) stay closed. Dependencies' licenses are checked by cargo-deny (`deny.toml`, `just deny`, CI); third-party notices are in `notices/` (`just notices`: cargo-about for the Rust crates, the web client's npm packages, llama.cpp; a hand-kept part for the bundled app libraries, the soundfont and the model downloaded at run time), checked fresh by `just check` and CI.

*Open:* the field and wording of the tier-3 grant; what a later service worker would cache.

### 2.25 Reading everything: places, efforts, digests, the briefing (plan)

Decided 2026-11-11, with the user, who asked for a client to "read everything" in (~1000 threads on gamer; finding the threads of one project was a hunt or a search) and thought of a 2D/3D map over memory's embeddings, eventually on Vision Pro. The first plan (place grouping, a timeline, digests, a UMAP canvas, a visionOS renderer) was put to three other models (GPT Astra, Gemini Argon, Fable 5.2, without tools); they agreed with each other more than with it, and the plan below is the result. **The bottleneck is absorption, not navigation:** a layout of 1000 threads does not make them readable; what reduces reading comes first.

**Principles.**
- **The unit is the effort, not the thread:** a conversation (a compaction chain, §2.15) with the threads spawned under it is one thing to read, with one digest and one read state. Places group efforts.
- **Reading everything means accounting for everything, not reading every token:** every effort has a read cursor (what the user has seen of it), and every view can say what changed since. "Seen in a digest" and "source read" are told apart.
- **Derived text always carries its source:** each item of a digest names the message it comes from, one click away. A summary without provenance is distrusted after its first mistake.
- **Relations have reasons:** "both edited `sync/reconcile.rs` yesterday", never "similarity 0.84". Hard edges (shared paths, commits, URLs, thread IDs, read from tool calls, not guessed by a model) come before semantic ones, and work for places out of memory.
- **No global embedding map** (decided against: UMAP's axes mean nothing, positions move when it is fitted again, transcripts cluster by wording more than by subject, and prior art, Obsidian's global graph and embedding atlases, is looked at and not used). Spatial views are a geography the user arranges by hand and that stays put, plus the local neighbourhood of the thing open.
- **Not engram:** engram forgets on purpose and places with `memory = false` have no embeddings (§2.9). Digests and the artefact index are a derived index of their own. **Decided 2026-11-11, by the user:** every place is digested and indexed, `memory = false` or not (a digest is not memory: nothing of it is recalled into another thread unasked, and engram still observes nothing there); every view that shows such a place's or thread's digest, relations or briefing items says that its memory is off (a mark on the place's section and page, and on the effort). Digests, the artefact index's facts and read cursors are synced records: computed once, by the node that runs the effort, and every node has them.

**Steps**, each useful alone, in order:

1. **Places, efforts, read state.** The web client's sidebar grouped by place (projects, folders, global; collapsible, the choice kept; counts and a mark for what needs the user), a place filter, a place page. `Request::Threads` takes a `place` so a small project's old threads are not hidden by the window (§2.24: 300 of ~1000). A read cursor per effort, synced (a record in the home workspace, as a pin is), advanced by opening or by marking read; "unread" and "changed since" in every list. The app follows.
2. **Digests and the artefact index** (node). A digest per effort, structured: goal, outcome, decisions, findings, artefacts changed, open questions, what it wants from the user; each item anchored to a message; incremental (per sealed segment, rolled up), by the quick model, with its freshness shown. The artefact index: paths, commits, URLs and thread IDs each thread touched, extracted deterministically from tool calls; ubiquitous ones (a README, `Cargo.lock`) weigh little.
3. **The briefing.** "Since you last looked": by place, then effort, a few lines each of what was decided, found, shipped, blocked or failed; keyboard-driven (next, expand to the source, mark read); ordered by surprise (cost or duration far from its siblings', a digest drifting from its goal, retries, claims not verified). Its drill-down is a skim mode of the transcript: tool calls folded to one-line outcomes, keys to jump between decisions. On the phone too.
4. **Related, with reasons.** A panel on the open effort: its spawn and report neighbours, efforts sharing artefacts, the nearest by digest embedding, each with its reason. The artefact pivot (a file, commit or URL: every thread that touched it, in order). Alerts: two efforts editing one file the same day; a claim contradicting another effort's. Later: the claims and decisions as records of their own (a ledger), durable questions over them ("what threatens the release?"), and the same notes given to the agent at work.
5. **Time and space.** A timeline aggregated first (place × day, drilling down to an effort's lanes with idle time folded; spawns as branches, compactions as joints). Then a 2D desk: places as landmarks the user arranges, efforts on them (size cost, state by colour), the neighbourhood of the selection drawn on demand. The visionOS app renders the same desk as a room: flat panels to read in, places around the user, depth for focus (the selected effort near, its relations behind), never for time.

**Step 2 as designed** (2026-11-11; after a survey of what the node has: no hook per sealed segment, per-turn hooks after a turn in `node.rs` (memory, titles); background model calls record no usage; `set`s read per thread are too slow for a list of 1000; tool calls are `Event::ToolCall { name, input }` and `ToolResult` in segments; a message's durable name is `<thread>#<msg id>`, the transcript's `n` is not).

- **The artefact index is derived, not written** (facts sync, not state, as §2.9): every node holds every segment, so each fills its own index table from the tool calls as it indexes a sealed segment, as `usage` is filled; nothing new syncs, and a rebuilt index gives the same rows. A row: thread, kind, value, verb, instant, the message it belongs to. Kinds and where they are read, by rule, never by a model: `path` (`edit_file` and `write_file`: wrote; `view_file`: read; as the call gave it, made absolute against the thread's directory when relative, `~` for the home directory), `url` (`browse_web`, `web_eval`, the browser tools' navigations: read; without fragment), `commit` (a `shell_run` result's `git commit` line, `[branch abc1234] subject`: wrote, with the subject), `thread` (`spawn`'s result, `send`, `wait`: spawned or told). Paths named only inside shell commands are not parsed in this version. `Request::Artefacts { thread }` (a conversation's, the chain's threads together, grouped by kind, latest first) and `Request::Touched { kind, value }` (every conversation that touched it, in time order, with verb and instant). Known limit: one file in two worktrees or on two nodes is two paths; folding them (by place and path within it) is open. Ubiquity is weighed where relations are drawn (step 4), not here.
- **A digest is a synced record of kind `digest`**, one per thread, a newer one superseding the last, written by the node that ran the turn (as titles are; a thread that moves is digested by its new runner from the last digest on). Fields: `thread`; `upto` (the last message it covers, `<thread>#<id>`); `model`; `usd` (what it cost, summed over its updates); `headline` (one line: where the thread stands); `items`, each `{ kind, text, at }` with `kind` one of `goal`, `outcome`, `decision`, `finding`, `change`, `question` (open), `ask` (what it wants from the user), and `at` the message it comes from. **Anchors are checked, not trusted:** an item whose `at` is not a message given to the model in that update or kept from the previous digest is dropped before writing. A conversation's digest is its newest thread's (a compaction's first update starts from the earlier thread's digest, so anchors into earlier threads stay).
- **Incremental:** an update gives the model the previous digest and what the thread said since `upto` (users' and the model's messages; tool calls as their one-line summaries, results cut short; the artefact rows of those messages), and asks for the whole digest again, at most 12 items, the superseded ones dropped. A first digest of a long thread goes through it in windows, the same way.
- **When:** after a turn ends (the per-turn hook), in the background, when the thread is not working again and said at least a few hundred words since `upto`; a thread is not digested more than once in ten minutes (the later turn's update covers both). **Backfill** of what exists is a sweep of its own, newest activity first, on the node that owns each thread (a phone's by another node, as loose ends are), within the day's cap.
- **Cost is said and capped:** `[digests] enabled` (default true), `model` (default the quick model), `daily_cap_usd` (default 2; the sweep and the per-turn updates stop for the day when reached, said once in the log and in `stitch node status`). Each update's cost is in the record; the day's total is node-local. **Every place is digested**, `memory = false` or not (decided above); a thread whose memory is off is digested too.
- **Read in one query:** the index keeps a table of each thread's latest digest (headline, counts of open questions and asks, `upto`, when), so the list of threads carries `digest: { headline, asks, questions, at, fresh }` per row (`fresh` false when the thread said more since) at no cost per row; `Request::Digest { thread }` gives the whole one, each item with the thread and message to open. The web client shows the headline under a row's title in place of the last words when there is one, and the digest at the top of a conversation, folded, each item opening its message (`openHit`); stale ones say so.
- **Not in this step:** roll-up to efforts (a parent's digest over its spawned threads') and to place and day, the digest's embedding (the encoder is not on every node: step 4 decides), the briefing (step 3).

*As built:* step 1's first part (local-56): `Request::Threads` takes `place` (a row's `place`, the label; the limit applies after the filter) and the reply gains `places: [{place, count}]` over every listed conversation, not the window's (the user's own list only, `every`; a thread with no record here counts under `?`). The web client's sidebar groups by place: pinned first in a section of their own, then one collapsible section per place by its latest activity (header: name, count, a dot when something in it needs the user or works), spawn trees and order within a section as before (`sidebar/groups.ts`, pure, `test/groups.test.ts`); open/closed kept in `localStorage` (`stitch.places`; 2026-11-17: a ▤/◷ button beside collapse-all swaps to the app's grouping, Today, Yesterday and Earlier by each tree's latest activity over the loaded threads, rows saying their place, kept as `stitch.sideOrder`; `groupByTime`), default open where something waits or works and for the latest place; an open section the window does not cover reads its place with `Threads { place }` (500 at most) and keeps it fresh on each list refresh; the text filter stays flat across sections and hides those with no match. Tested: `the_threads_list_counts_places_and_filters_by_one`. **Memory off** (local-56): a `places` entry carries `memory: false` only when the place's settings say `memory = false` (§2.9, `Node::place_memory_off`); the section header shows a quiet ∅ with the tooltip "memory off" (a single thread's switch is not shown yet). **Cost:** the places count is computed in one pass over `listed_threads`' own rows (`Place::of_thread` with `places()` read once, no record read per thread); measured with 600 threads, a list of 300 rows takes ~200 ms steady (first request ~430 ms) with and without it, so the count is within noise (~5–10 ms), the rows being the cost (`list_timing`, an ignored test). **Read state, per conversation** (local-57): `Request::Read { thread, upto }` writes a user `set` of `read` (an RFC 3339 instant) on the newest thread of the chain, as `Pin` does; a chain's cursor is the latest `read` on any of its threads (`lineage_value`, `meta::read_cursor`), and a `Read` that does not move it forward writes nothing (these records sync). The cursor is a **row's `last_step`** (the step the user was shown), not the clock, so "changed since" is exact: a row is `unread` when its `last_step` is after the cursor, and never read is unread (`Read` without `upto` reads to the latest activity now). Not told apart: the user's own step (a message typed in the TUI) also moves `last_step`, so it shows unread until a client reads it. The web client reads the open conversation when its end is in view and the window is focused (`readstate.ts`; the first read of a thread at once, later ones debounced 3 s / 30 s while a turn streams); unread rows have a bold title and a small dot, a section header shows its unread count and a hover ✓ that marks its loaded unread conversations read. Tested: `a_conversation_is_unread_after_its_cursor`, the compaction test (chain cursor), `readstate.test.ts`. **Typing is reading** (local-58): where the node accepts the user's message into a conversation (`Request::Chat`, not a resume or an app's end user; `Request::Send` from the user, which is what the web client and the app send), `meta::read_on_send` writes the cursor to the conversation's latest activity *before* the message, only when that moves it (one record per message at most). The index has no per-row "last user message" (a user's message is written into the segment of the step that answers it, so it is not a `last_step` of its own), which is why the write is at the accept point; the reply is after the cursor, so it shows unread if the user is not looking. Not covered: a message woven into a running turn on the chat connection (`Request::Interject`, the TUI's), whose thread the handler does not hold; the user is looking there. **`ReadAll { place }`** writes **one floor record**, not one per conversation (revised after the first version wrote a record per conversation, 1000 synced records for one click): `read-floor` (`meta::READ_FLOOR`, a kind of the user's in the home workspace as the APNs `device` records are, no store change), fields `place` (`all`, or a place's **key**; a label from the client is resolved node-side to the key(s) of the places with that name) and `upto`, the latest listed activity at the time of the request (not the clock, so a step landing later stays unread); the greatest `upto` per key counts, so two nodes writing at once agree and an older one never lowers it. A conversation's effective cursor is the max of its own `read`, its place's floor and the global floor (`meta::cursor_of`; the floors are read once per `Threads` request, `Floors::read`); `Read` and the write on send also stop when a floor already covers. The reply is how many conversations it read (those unread before); nothing unread writes nothing. The sidebar's top "✓ all read" (confirm) and each section's ✓ use it, so they cover rows not loaded. **`Opened` and `Read` overlap** (`Opened` is loose ends' "seen at" time, written when the user opens a thread that moved; `Read` is the per-conversation cursor, read through the chain, written by a step the user was shown): both are kept for now, and folding them into one cursor is open. Not yet: the roll-up per effort (spawned threads with their parent), the "changed since" views, place pages, digests.

*As built, step 2, the artefact index* (local-59; `stitch-store/src/artefacts.rs`, `index.rs`, `stitch-node/src/artefacts.rs`; digests: next paragraph). **Table:** `artefacts(segment, thread, kind, value, verb, first_at, last_at, n, first_msg, last_msg, subject)`, primary key `(segment, kind, value, verb)`, indexes on `thread` and on `(kind, value)`. It is filled in `insert_segment` in the same transaction as `usage` (so by `refresh` on every node, by a rebuild, and removed with its segment in `remove_path`), by `artefacts::segment_rows(&Segment)`, a pure function of the segment: calls are paired with their results by ID (a step is written whole), rows deduped **per segment** by `(kind, value, verb)` with first and last instant, a count and the messages. `Index` `SCHEMA` 3 → 4: an index of another version is reset and refilled from the roots at open, as the bump to 3 did (no back-fill code); STORE_SHAPE/STORE_FORMAT are untouched (`segment.rs` is not changed). **Extraction** is `artefacts::extract(tool, input, Option<(ok, output)>)`: `edit_file`/`write_file` `path` wrote (only with a result, so a write not known to have happened counts for nothing); `view_file` `path` read; `browse_web`, `web_eval`, `browser_navigate` and `browser_tabs` with `action: new`, `url` read, without fragment, http(s) only (`back`, `reload`, `file:` are not URLs); a `shell_run` result's text scanned line by line for git's `[branch abc1234] subject`, also `[branch (root-commit) abc1234]` and `[detached HEAD abc1234]` (hash 7–40 hex, lowercased; subject cut at 200 chars), commit wrote with the subject; `spawn`'s result, `Spawned thread <id>`, thread spawned; `send`'s `thread`, thread told; `wait` nothing. A failed call (`ok: false`) gives nothing at all (not only for writes: a failed read or navigation did not read). **Deviations:** (1) the commit scan also covers the result of `shell_send`, `shell_wait` and `job_output`, where a commit run in a session or a background job shows up; (2) **paths are kept as the call gave them**: not made absolute and not `~`-folded. A relative path's directory is not in the records the same on every node (a project thread's directory is a node's checkout, `$HOME` is the node's), and the index must come out the same everywhere; so `src/a.rs` of two threads is one row value whatever their directories (a place-aware fold is the "two worktrees" limit above, open); (3) a message is the transcript's local ID (`m12`, the latest `msg` event before the call; none when the segment starts with a call), stored as such and given as the durable `<thread>#<id>` by the requests; (4) a tool output that is a blob reference (large outputs) has no text here, so a commit in one is missed. Not read: other workers' tools (`Read`, `Edit` of Claude Code), paths named inside shell commands. **Requests:** `Request::Artefacts { thread }` → `{thread (the chain's newest), chain, kinds: {path, url, commit, thread: [{value, verb, first_at, last_at, n, msg, first_msg, thread, subject}]}}`, the chain's threads together (counts add, the latest touch's message wins), one item per `(value, verb)`, latest first; `Request::Touched { kind, value }` → `{kind, value, conversations: [{thread, title, place, verb, first_at, last_at, n, msg, first_msg, subject}]}`, a chain folded to its newest thread, one entry per conversation and verb, deleted threads left out, in time order of the first touch; the value is normalised as the index keeps it (a URL's fragment dropped; `back` or a non-http URL is an error), an unknown kind is an error. Both are list reads (`list_read`, gated on the web socket). Types: `Request.ts` by `just client-types`, replies by hand in `payload.ts` (`Artefacts`, `Touched`); CLI `stitch artefacts <thread>` and `stitch touched <value> [--kind …]` (kind guessed from the value). **Measured** on gamer's data (22,709 segments over 29 roots, 84,164 usage rows, release build): a full index build from the roots takes 12.6 s with the table (the cost is parsing the files; the extraction is a pass over events already in memory), 12,975 artefact rows (path 9,805 over 5,642 values, url 965, thread 2,173, commit 32) in 922 threads, at most 177 rows in one thread; deduping per thread instead of per segment would give 9,428 rows, so per segment was kept (it is the unit `usage` and removal use). The table and its two indexes take 5.5 MB of the 70 MB index. `artefact_sizes` (ignored, `STITCH_HOME_COPY=<roots, colon-separated>`) measures it again. Tested: unit tests for each rule and edge case (`artefacts::tests`), `tests/artefacts.rs` (two requests over a compaction chain, failed call, fragment, deleted thread, one conversation touching a thread with two verbs).

*As built, step 2, digests, part 1* (local-60; `stitch-node/src/digests.rs`, `stitch-store/src/index.rs`; the backfill sweep and the web client's UI are not built). **Record:** kind `digest` (`stitch_store::DIGEST_KIND`, no store change: STORE_SHAPE/STORE_FORMAT untouched), in the thread's repository, authored by the node, fields `thread`, `upto` (`<thread>#<id>`), `step` (see deviations), `model`, `usd` (summed over the thread's own updates), `headline`, `items` (`[{kind, text, at}]`), refs `thread` (so deleting the thread purges its digests: `purge::goes_with`) and `supersedes` (the thread's previous digest). **Table:** `digests(thread PRIMARY KEY, record, created, upto, step, headline, asks, questions)` plus an index on `record`, index `SCHEMA` 4 → 5 (reset and refilled from the roots at open); `insert_record` upserts a `digest` record's row when it is newer than the one standing (by `created`, then ID: one indexed late from another node does not replace), `remove_path` deletes the row with its record; `Index::digests()` reads every row in one query, `Index::digest(thread)` one. **Update** (`update_with`, testable with a scripted `Asker`; the real one is `Provided`, one provider call through `resolve_authenticated`, priced with `pricing::priced`): the thread's transcript (`chat::transcript_shown`) becomes *groups*, a message with the tool calls under it, labelled `[<thread>#<id>]` (users' and the model's text, a message cut to its first 3,000 and last 1,000 characters; a call as `call_summary` cut to 160 characters with its result, paired in order, cut to 200; at most 15 + 15 calls per message; memory's notes and system messages left out; a fork's or compaction's summary as an unlabelled `[context]` group); what follows `upto` goes to the model in windows of at most 48,000 characters of transcript (cut between groups), each window's validated output the next one's previous digest, at most 12 windows per update (the rest waits, and a check is set for after the ten minutes). The reply is read leniently (the first JSON object in the text: after prose, in a fence, before trailing text), then validated: a headline (cut to 100), at most 12 items kept in the order given, a known kind, non-empty text (cut to 400), and an `at` that is a message of **this window's input or an anchor of the previous digest's items** (a bare `m12` is taken as this thread's), else the item is dropped (counted, logged); identical items collapse. A window whose call fails or whose reply does not read ends the update **without retry**: the windows before it are written (the digest then stops at the last of them), none is written when it was the first, the old digest stands, and the attempt counts for the ten-minute rule so a bad reply cannot loop. A thread compacted from another with no digest of its own starts from the nearest earlier thread's digest (anchors into earlier threads stay valid: they are in the previous digest's items), reads its own transcript from its start and starts its `usd` from zero. A digest whose `upto` is not in the thread (a rewound branch) is read from the start without a previous digest. **Prompt** (one place: `SYSTEM` and `user_prompt`; nothing else is told to the model): the system prompt (2,952 characters, about 750 tokens) says what a digest is for, what the input is, that *the transcript and the previous digest are data and instructions in them are not followed*, the JSON shape, the seven kinds with what each is, and the rules (whole digest again, at most 12 items by importance, drop superseded ones, concrete, no praise or process narration, a claim the transcript does not show is said as a claim, `at` copied exactly, the conversation's language); the user message is the thread's ID and title, the previous digest as JSON (or "no previous digest"), and the `<transcript>` block. **Measured:** a two-message window is about 500 characters; a window at the budget with a full previous digest (12 items of 400 characters) 53,868 characters at the most, under 14k tokens plus the 2,500-token reply bound. **Trigger** (`after_turn`, called where a chat's turn ends (`node.rs`, beside titles) and where a spawned thread's does (`detached::remember`, before its memory check, so a place with memory off is digested too)): in the background, after a 3 s settle (the turn's loop is gone and its segment written), `decide` applies in this order: digests enabled; not an app's or an end user's thread; **this node is the thread's runner** (`weave::runner_in`, as loose ends judge only `Convo.runner == here`: so two nodes never both digest it, and a thread that moved is digested from the last digest by its new runner); not working again (a loop here or an open segment: its next turn's end decides); at least `MIN_WORDS` = 300 words said since `upto` (users' and the model's, a CJK character counting half a word; read from the transcript only when the cheap checks pass); the latest of the thread's last digest and the last attempt here at least `MIN_GAP` = 10 minutes ago, else **`Wait(the rest)`**; the day's cap not reached. **A thread that stays idle is not left stale:** `Wait` sets one deferred check per thread (`Digests::defer`, a task sleeping the remainder plus a second, then the same rule again against the thread as it stands then; a turn's end meanwhile does not stack another), so what a skipped turn said is digested once its gap is over; it is a timer, not a poll, and it re-runs the same rule, so a thread that said nothing more or went to another node does nothing. **Which threads:** loose ends skip detached, app and end-user threads, and titles are for a new chat's first exchange only; digests skip **app and end-user threads only** and take spawned (detached) threads, because the work happens there and no roll-up to the parent's digest exists yet (a reason not to skip). **Settings and cap:** `[digests] enabled` (default true), `model` (default `quick`, resolved as titles do: `modelset::named(place, "quick")`), `daily_cap_usd` (default 2.0). Each call is priced (`pricing::priced`) and added to the record's `usd` and to a node-local day total (`index.set_local("digests-day", "<date> <usd>")`, the node's local calendar date; as loose ends keeps its pass total); the cap is checked before each call, so a run can overshoot by one call, and when reached no update runs (said once a day in the log); a model with no known price counts as zero and is logged. **Reads:** `live::threads` reads the table once per request and adds `digest: {headline, asks, questions, at, fresh}` to each row (absent when none; a conversation's own newest thread's, else the latest of its earlier threads'; `fresh` = the digest's `step` is not before the row's `last_step`), no query per row; `Request::Digest { thread, update? }` → `{thread, digest: {record, thread, headline, items: [{kind, text, at, thread, msg}], model, usd, upto, at, fresh} | null}` (a list read, gated on the web socket when it does not update) and with `update: true` one update first (not held to the ten minutes or the words; held to the cap and to the node being the runner) and `update: {outcome (written, nothing new, capped, failed), why, usd, model, windows, more, dropped, day_usd, cap_usd}`; `stitch digest <thread> [--update]` prints it. Types: `Request.ts` by `just client-types`, `RowDigest`, `Digest` in `payload.ts`. **Deviations from "Step 2 as designed":** (1) the record has one more field, `step` (the thread's latest segment instant when its transcript was read), because `fresh` is "no activity after what the digest read", which the message ID `upto` alone cannot say without an instant; (2) the table keeps the latest digest per thread only (an upsert), not every record's; (3) the artefact rows of the messages are **not** given to the model: the tool-call summaries already name the paths, URLs and commits, and a second source would be one more thing to keep consistent; (4) no line in `stitch node status` (it would add a field to `Reply::Status`); `stitch digest --update` prints today's total against the cap instead; (5) the ten-minute rule counts a failed attempt, so an unreadable reply is not retried by the next turn inside ten minutes; (6) a thread that never said 300 words since its digest is not digested by the trigger (the backfill sweep and `stitch digest --update` do it); (7) the day total lives in the index's local `meta`, so a rebuild of the index (a schema bump) resets it: a cap is a guard against a runaway, not accounting (the records' `usd` are). **Tested:** `digests::tests` (words; groups with calls, results and cuts; windows; what follows `upto`; reading and validating replies; a first digest as one call with the transcript marked as data; an update with the previous digest and only the new part, kept and dropped items, invalid anchors; a backlog in windows each the next one's previous, with the summed cost; an unreadable reply and a failed call leaving the progress so far and not retried; the cap between windows; an unpriced model; the trigger rule case by case, including the runner, the gap's remainder and the cap; one deferred check per thread; `fresh`; a row's digest from the earlier thread of a chain; prompt sizes), `stitch-store/tests/digests.rs` (the table: filled, replaced by a newer one and not by an older, rebuilt equal, gone with the thread), and `tests/digests.rs` on a node against a mock model (first digest, the row's digest and `fresh`, the thread moving, an update with the previous digest and the summed cost, an unreadable reply leaving the old digest, the cap, the background trigger after a long turn and not after a short one, no second update inside ten minutes, a compacted thread starting from the earlier thread's digest). Not tested end to end: two nodes (the runner rule is tested as a predicate, and `update` refuses a thread another node runs), and the deferred check firing after ten real minutes (its remainder is tested, its one-per-thread rule too).

*As built, step 2, digests, part 2: phones and the sweep* (local-61). **Phones do not digest:** `digests::responsible` asks loose ends' own rule, `loose::Judge::judges` (a `Convo::bare` with the thread's runner and latest step; `loose::judge_here` is now `pub(crate)`): a thread this node runs is its own, a thread a phone runs is the first non-phone node up's at once and a later one's after its rank's failover (90 minutes quiet each), and a node that is itself in the phones list digests nothing; `enabled()` is also false on a mobile build (`cfg!(mobile)`), so no digest call, timer or sweep starts on a phone. The trigger's "runner here" fact and `update`'s refusal both use it (a user asking by `stitch digest --update` is not held to the failover wait). Tested as a predicate (`a_phones_threads_are_digested_by_the_node_loose_ends_would_judge_them_on`). **The sweep** (`digests::sweep`, `run`): `digests::run` is spawned with the node's other timers (loose ends' shape): five minutes after the start, then hourly, each time `survey` first. `survey` is free (index facts only): every listed conversation that is not an app's or an end user's, deleted, or without a step, with where its digest stands (fresh, stale, none: the newest thread's, else an earlier thread's of the chain) and whether this node is `responsible`; due are those not fresh, this node's, not working (a loop or open segment), not being updated (`Digests::busy`: the trigger, a manual update and the sweep share the one per-thread guard, and `update_with` answers `Done::Busy` rather than a second call), not tried in the last ten minutes (the trigger's gap, so a thread digested minutes ago is the trigger's), and not set aside at its present step; newest latest step first. With nothing due nothing else happens: no provider is resolved, no model asked. Each due thread is then read (`worth`, no model): with no digest, **an answered exchange is enough however short** (a user message with a model message after it: a short thread is digested once), with one (its own or an earlier thread's in its chain) the words said since `upto` must reach the 300-word floor; a thread that does not is *set aside* at its step (`Digests::skipped`, in memory) and not read again until it moves. Taken one at a time, with `[digests] backfill_pause_secs` (default 5) between two calls, through `update` (so the cap is checked before every call and the sweep stops, said once a day, when it is reached), until the node stops: the loop waits on the node's shutdown watch, and a call in flight is dropped. **Busy:** `Shared::busy` (what delays a restart) counts loops, chats starting, tool calls for other nodes and shells; a digest call is none of them, so a sweep in progress never holds a restart, and a stop drops its one call. `[digests] backfill` (default true) turns the timer off; `stitch digest --sweep` still runs one by hand. **Status:** `Request::DigestStatus { sweep? }` (local-61) → `{enabled, backfill, conversations, fresh, stale, none, due_here, day_usd, cap_usd, running, last_sweep, sweep?}`, `stitch digest` with no thread (or `--status`) prints it, `--sweep` runs one sweep now and reports it. **Deviations:** the sweep takes the ten-minute gap too (so a stale short thread digested minutes ago waits for the trigger or the next hour), `skipped` is in memory (a restart reads the stale ones again once, a transcript read each, no model), and the sweep's model is the quick model per thread's place as the trigger's (one `update` each). **Tested** (mock model): newest first with one call each, short threads digested once and a fresh sweep asking nothing, a stale short thread under the floor set aside with no call and not due again, the cap stopping a sweep after one call and keeping it stopped, a thread being updated (its call held) left to the update while the sweep takes the other, once each.

*As built, step 2, digests, part 2: the web client* (`apps/client/src/digest/`, `sidebar/Sidebar.tsx`, `app.tsx`, `actions/MsgActions.tsx`, `mock.ts`). **Row:** when a row has `digest`, its headline replaces the last-words line (the last words are the row button's tooltip); a stale one (`fresh: false`) is dimmer and italic with the tooltip "stale: the conversation moved since this digest"; `asks > 0` adds a small outlined "asks" chip after the title (tooltip "asks you N thing(s)"), apart from the state and unread dots. **Block:** `DigestBlock` is the first child of the transcript, folded to the headline, a counts line (`counts`: "1 ask · 2 open · 3 decisions · …", asks first, the goal not counted) and a "stale" hint; unfolded it groups the items in the order ask, question, outcome, decision, finding, change, goal (`grouped`, `KIND_ORDER`; each group in the order the node gave, most important first), each item's text a button that opens its message, then a footer with model, cost, "as of <age>", "stale: the conversation moved since", and an **Update** button (`Request::Digest { update: true }`) whose outcome shows beside it (`said`: written with cost and windows, nothing new, the cap reached with the day's total, busy, or the failure's reason). The fold is the component's own state (keyed by conversation, not persisted); the digest is read when a conversation opens and read again when its row's `digest.at` changes (`digest/state.ts`). **Opening a message, fixed properly:** the transcript lacked a durable name on earlier threads' messages (`id` is `None` there on purpose: IDs are per thread and a fork or rewind would take it for the newest's). The node now adds `mid` (`<thread>#<id>`) to every message of the lineage's own threads (not to what a fork inherits, which is its source's), and `Msg` marks each with `data-did`, also when the earlier part is read as it was; an item opens by `openItem`: the item's thread first when it is not in the open conversation's chain (`inConversation`), then scrolls to `[data-did]` (looking for it for 3 s while the transcript reads) and marks it 4 s. `data-mid` and `openHit` are untouched. Tested: `compacted_thread_starts_from…` also reads the transcript and checks the earlier thread's `mid` and that its `id` is still null. **Mock and checks:** `?mock` has digests (a fresh one with an ask and open questions, a stale one whose update runs into the cap, a fresh one without asks, one item anchored to another thread); `?mock&open=<thread>` opens a thread and `&unfold` unfolds its digest (for screenshots; the bundle is served over HTTP and read by `browse_web`, which has no Chrome install to drive here). Client tests: `test/digest.test.ts` (order, empty groups, counts line, which thread to open, outcome wording, the asks tooltip). **Reading pass (2026-11-11):** a row's headline (or last words) is 13 px, up to 3 lines (line-clamp), full-ink when fresh, dimmer italic when stale; titles up to 2 lines; the meta line stays one line; the asks chip is the needs-you orange. The sidebar is resizable by its right edge (`sidebar/width.ts`: 260–640 px, `stitch.sidebar` in localStorage, double-click or no stored value gives the default, 360 above 1400 px window width else 320, arrows resize the focused grip; hidden at ≤720 px, where the CSS variable `--side-w` is ignored by the narrow layout). Hovering a row 400 ms (or keyboard focus-visible; never touch/pen) shows a card beside the sidebar (title, headline, `rowCounts` asks/open from the row, goal's first 4 lines, place · node · state · cost, times); no digest fetch; moving between rows switches at once, leaving hides after 120 ms, scroll/Esc/resize hide it, it takes no pointer events. The digest block starts unfolded when it holds an ask or the conversation was unread when opened (`unfoldByDefault`), items at 14 px/1.5, kinds as labelled chips (ask orange, open muted yellow, others neutral), 75ch wide. Mock-only `?mock&hover=<thread>` shows a row's card. Tests: `test/reading.test.ts`.

*As built, the Atlas prototype* (2026-11-11; an app in a scratch directory, not in the repository, over `stitch.live.threads`: Islands, River and Space views of the whole forest, efforts per place). What it showed on the user's real data (gamer's index: 1,580 threads, 1,121 detached and 459 conversations; 722 of the 1,083 spawn edges have a conversation as their parent): the apps' listing with `all` gave detached threads only and at most 500, so most efforts had no root and their children drew as orphans. Hence `conversations: true` above, and Atlas now says when a parent is not loaded instead of making a root of the child. The map goes into the client next (steps 5's desk, by place, with efforts as its unit).

*As built, the map in the web client* (2026-11-12, local-62; branch `reading`; `apps/client/src/map/`; Islands only). **Data:** `Request::Threads { forest: true }` (the same listing and code path as the client's own list, `live::threads` with `conversations=1` added to the query: every conversation and thread, compaction chains folded, lean rows, `limit` up to 5000); `Api.listForest()` asks for 5000. Read when the map is first shown (`map/state.ts`), and again on the same debounced change signal the list follows (`store.changeTick`) only while the map is on screen (else it is marked stale and read when shown); a reading whose rows' signature (id, state, last step, unread, question, title, cost, place, parent, digest) equals the last one's builds nothing. Local protocol 61 → 62 (`API_SHAPE`, `Request.ts`). Tested: `the_forest_lists_every_conversation_as_lean_rows`. **Model** (`map/model.ts`, pure, `test/map.test.ts`): ported from the prototype's `common.js`: rows → threads → efforts (a root and all it spawned, however deep, across places) → places; weights by cost, else calls × the median cost per call; the roll-up of need/working/unread/cost/times; a parent that is not listed makes its children orphans under a synthetic "(parent not loaded)" effort per place (not counted as a thread); cycles cut at the earliest member (the prototype cut the wrong end of the walk's chain: fixed, tested); deterministic order (efforts by start then ID, places by key; the result does not depend on the order of the rows); the filters fade (search words, needs you, time) and the time filter *removes* efforts and places with nothing in the window, re-packing with the animated glide; unread marks automatic (off above 40% unread). **View** (`map/islands.ts`, `MapView.tsx`, `Card.tsx`): the canvas engine is the prototype's `islands.js` (d3 circle packing, zoom and pan, semantic zoom, labels that never overlap or go under 10 px, click to dive into a place or effort, click empty space to go out, edges of the hovered or selected effort, the working halo, needs-you markers), with `devicePixelRatio`; the selection, hover, filters and model are signals (`map/state.ts`) the engine reads through an interface, so it does not depend on the component. Card: title, place · node · state · cost · times, the digest's headline or last words, the goal's first lines, an **Open** button, the approval's question with Allow / Always / Decline (`S.answer`), the spawned tree with counts folded at 30 whose rows select (Open on each, double-click opens); a place's card lists what needs you and the recent efforts. A hover previews only while nothing is selected (a selected card stays where its buttons are). Single click selects; double-click (or Enter on the focused canvas) opens the selection (the first click's selection stands, since its fly is under way; its second click does not zoom out). **Back-restore:** the map is mounted the first time it is shown and then kept mounted, hidden, while a conversation shows (`hidden`, `display: none`): its canvas, transform, focus, layout, hover and the signals (filter, search, selection, card) are untouched, so returning is instant and exact, with no relayout; the engine ignores resizes to 0 while hidden and draws nothing; a change of rows while hidden is read when it is shown again. **Two-way sync:** the conversation open in the client is drawn on the map with a dashed mint ring; opening a conversation from anywhere makes it the map's selection (a sidebar click opens the conversation as before and the map eases to it when shown again; what the map itself opened is already selected, so nothing moves). **Mock and tools:** `?mock` generates 460 rows over 14 places with spawn trees (`mock.ts`, seeded); `&instant` skips the animations, `&mapsel=place:<key>|effort:<id|bigN>|thread:<id|needN>`, `&maptime=`, `&mapq=`, `&mapneeds` set a state, and `window.__atlas` (mock only) exposes the transform and a pick for checks. **Not ported:** River (deprioritised by the user, 2026-11-12: he barely used it; not to be ported for now), the prototype's tab bar (the view is the map alone) and its progressive loading (one request). The prototype's source is at `apps/client/tools/atlas-prototype/` for reference (the one place under `tools/` that fits: it holds the client's dev tooling), with a README saying what became what.

*As built, Space: the map in 3D* (2026-11-12, local-63; branch `reading`; `apps/client/src/map/space/`). A second mode of the map, the **2D | 3D** switch in its header (remembered in `stitch.mapView`; the route says it too, `#/map/3d`, and `#/map` is 2D; back from a conversation returns to the mode it was opened from), sharing everything with Islands through the map's state: the forest and the effort roll-up, search, the time filter (which removes, and Space re-lays out with it, gliding), needs-you and unread, the selection and hover, the detail card with Open, approvals and the spawn tree, the two-way sync with the sidebar. three.js 0.187 (exact pin) in a lazily loaded chunk (§2.24); no OrbitControls: a small orbit/pan/zoom of its own (spherical camera around a target, easing, inertia), which keeps the chunk to what it draws. **Layout** (`layout.ts`, pure, `test/space.test.ts`): the ground plane *is* Islands' packing (`Islands.layout()`, its final positions; pack units ×1.3, centred), so each place's ring and each effort's root stand exactly where 2D draws them, the base for a later Vision Pro view; height is recency on a log-time scale (knee 6 h) over the span of what is shown (1.5 days to 4 months), with levels today, yesterday, 3 days, a week, a month, 3 months inside it. Each effort is its root with its spawned threads on a **radial tree** (each subtree a wedge proportional to its leaves, so edges do not cross; each ring's radius the larger of the ring inside plus a step and what its closest pair needs to be a gap apart, so no two nodes of a tree are ever closer than that), **scaled into its room**: the effort's packed radius plus half the clearance to its nearest neighbour, which no two efforts' rooms can share (tested on random circles and on the mock forests), spread over most of it (so trees read from afar) and never looser than a cap; nodes never wider than their spacing. Better than the prototype, whose trees took a fixed radius by thread count and overlapped their neighbours. **Drawing** (`engine.ts`, one draw call per kind, 6 in all: the threads as Points, the glow of what works or needs you (additive), marks (thin rings: selection, hover, the open conversation, unread), the edges (one instanced strip of 12 segments per edge, a quadratic curve widened in screen space, so it is continuous and fat, not segment quads that double at the joints), the ground (place rings, faint place columns, time-level rings: one LineSegments), the ground's disc). Every position has a from and a to, so a re-layout glides on the GPU through one uniform (new things fade in). Points: size attenuated by distance and clamped per kind in css px (spawned 2.4–16, lone 2.6–14, roots of trees 4.6–30, working 6–26, needs you 7–28): far nodes never vanish, near ones never blow up, and what works or needs you is never a speck and is drawn last, with a glow, so it is the loudest from any angle; **lone conversations are smaller and dimmer** than roots with trees, so structure stands out. Edges are the structure: wider and brighter for what moved in the last 10 days, orange or teal when the child needs you or works; the selected effort's edges brighter and wider and everything else dimmed (gently for a hover). **Selection** is a 1.5 px white ring 3 px outside the node's drawn size (it scales with the node, not a chunky disc); hover 1 px; the open conversation a mint ring; unread a 1 px hairline at 22% (only with the unread toggle on, and only in the focused effort when there is one). Fog fades with distance from the target. **Labels** are HTML over the canvas (crisp at any pixel ratio, faded in and out over ~110 ms, not popped): up to 20 effort titles chosen by size, recency, needs-you and working, every place's name on the near rim of its ring, all of the focused (selected, else hovered) effort's threads, the hovered thread always; placed greedily in screen space with 6 px between them, ones already shown keeping their place against slightly better newcomers (no flicker while orbiting). The time levels are an altimeter at the right of the view at the target's depth, placed first so titles keep clear of it. **Interaction:** drag orbits (inertia), right/middle/shift-drag pans along the ground, wheel and ctrl-wheel (trackpad pinch) zoom toward the cursor, two fingers pinch and pan; a tap selects (a root or another effort's thread also flies to frame its effort; a thread of the focused effort selects in place), a second tap or double-click or Enter opens, Esc clears (the map's), arrows orbit, shift-arrows pan, +/- zoom; the canvas is focusable and does not trap Tab. A selection made elsewhere (card, search, sidebar) flies there. With a card open, the projection is offset so what is selected centres in the space left of it. Top-down and Overview buttons. **Picking** projects the threads once per camera change (a typed array, also used by the labels), never raycasts; ~0.05 ms a pick at 1,560 threads. **Rendering on demand:** a frame only for a change, an animation (fly, inertia, glide, label fades), the working glow's pulse (14 fps, for 30 s after the last input) and the idle rotation (after 9 s quiet with nothing selected, stopping after three minutes, off with `prefers-reduced-motion`); nothing while hidden (a conversation, or 2D, on screen) or in a background tab; device pixel ratio capped at 2.
